[margin-top=-3.10pt]
=== ¶ The Temper.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=10.73pt]
HOw should I praise thee, Lord! how should my [.turn-down]##rymes##
Gladly engrave thy love in steel,
[.i215]##{nbsp}##If what my soul doth feel sometimes,
My soul might ever feel!

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
Al-[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 55 (leaf 54)
[.v,margin-top=-3.28pt]
Although there were some fourtie heav'ns, or more,
[.i400]##{nbsp}##Sometimes I peere above them all;
[.i360]##{nbsp}##Sometimes I hardly reach a score,
[.i465]##{nbsp}##Sometimes to hell I fall.

[.v,margin-top=7.33pt]
O rack me not to such a vast extent;
[.i270]##{nbsp}##Those distances belong to thee:
[.i270]##{nbsp}##The world's too little for thy tent,
[.i400]##{nbsp}##A grave too big for me.

[.v,margin-top=7.18pt]
Wilt thou meet arms with man, that thou dost stretch
[.i270]##{nbsp}##A crumme of dust from heav'n to hell?
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Will great God measure with a wretch?
[.i360]##{nbsp}##Shall he thy stature spell?

[.v,margin-top=7.17pt]
O let me, when thy roof my soul hath hid,
[.i165]##{nbsp}##O let me roost and nestle there:
[.i165]##{nbsp}##Then of a sinner thou art rid,
[.i360]##{nbsp}##And I of hope and fear.

[.v,margin-top=7.07pt]
Yet take thy way; for sure thy way is best:
[.i115]##{nbsp}##Stretch or contract me thy poore debter:
[.i115]##{nbsp}##This is but tuning of my breast,
[.i270]##{nbsp}##To make the musick better.

[.v,margin-top=7.36pt]
Whether I flie with angels, fall with dust,
[.i115]##{nbsp}##Thy hands made both, and I am there:
[.i115]##{nbsp}##Thy power and love, my love and trust
[.i270]##{nbsp}##Make one place ev'ry where.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=4.77pt]
'''

[margin-top=1.63pt]
=== ¶ The Temper.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=4.91pt]
IT cannot be. Where is that mightie joy,
Which just now took up all my heart?
[.i150]##{nbsp}##Lord, if thou must needs use thy dart,
Save that, and me; or sin for both destroy.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.10pt]
The[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 56 (leaf 55)
[.v,margin-top=2.80pt]
The grosser world stands to thy word and art;
[.i290]##{nbsp}##But thy diviner world of grace
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Thou suddenly dost raise and race,
And ev'ry day a new Creatour art.

[.v,margin-top=6.70pt]
O fix thy chair of grace, that all my powers
[.i195]##{nbsp}##May also fix their reverence:
[.i195]##{nbsp}##For when thou dost depart from hence,
They grow unruly, and sit in thy bowers.

[.v,margin-top=7.81pt]
Scatter, or binde them all to bend to thee:
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Though elements change, and heaven move,
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Let not thy higher Court remove,
But keep a standing Majestie in me.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=7.92pt]
'''

[margin-top=5.13pt]
=== ¶ Jordan.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=5.28pt]
WHo sayes that fictions onely and false hair
Become a verse? Is there in truth no beautie
Is all good structure in a winding stair?
May no lines passe, except they do their dutie
[.i175]##{nbsp}##Not to a true, but painted chair?

[.v,margin-top=7.18pt]
Is it no verse, except enchanted groves
And sudden arbours shadow course-spunne lines?
Must purling streams refresh a lovers loves?
Must all be vail'd, while he that reades, divines,
[.i70]##{nbsp}##Catching the sense at two removes?

[.v,margin-top=7.39pt]
Shepherds are honest people; let them sing:
Riddle who list, for me, and pull for Prime:
I envie no mans nightingale or spring;
Nor let them punish me with losse of ryme,
[.i175]##{nbsp}##Who plainly say, __My God, My King.__

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=3.24pt]
¶ Employ-[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 57 (leaf 56)
[margin-top=3.45pt]
=== ¶ Employment.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.97pt]
IF as a flowre doth spread and die,
Thou wouldst extend me to some good,
Before I were by frosts extremitie
[.i930]##{nbsp}##Nipt in the bud;

[.v,margin-top=11.49pt]
[.i110]##{nbsp}##The sweetnesse and the praise were thine;
[.i110]##{nbsp}##But the extension and the room,
[.i110]##{nbsp}##Which in thy garland I should fill, were mine
[.i930]##{nbsp}##At thy great doom.

[.v,margin-top=11.93pt]
[.i110]##{nbsp}##For as thou dost impart thy grace,
[.i110]##{nbsp}##The greater shall our glorie be.
[.i110]##{nbsp}##The measure of our joyes is in this place,
[.i930]##{nbsp}##The stuffe with thee.

[.v,margin-top=11.42pt]
Let me not languish then, and spend
[.i110]##{nbsp}##A life as barren to thy praise,
[.i110]##{nbsp}##As is the dust, to which that life doth tend,
[.i930]##{nbsp}##But with delaies.

[.v,margin-top=11.82pt]
[.i110]##{nbsp}##All things are busie; onely I
[.i110]##{nbsp}##Neither bring hony with the bees,
[.i110]##{nbsp}##Nor flowres to make that, nor the husbandrie
[.i930]##{nbsp}##To water these.

[.v,margin-top=11.38pt]
[.i110]##{nbsp}##I am no link of thy great chain,
[.i110]##{nbsp}##But all my companie is a weed.
Lord place me in thy consort; give one strain
[.i930]##{nbsp}##To my poore reed.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
¶ The[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1145]##{nbsp}##C

endif::[]

<<<

// page 58 (leaf 57)
[margin-top=3.81pt]
=== ¶ The H. Scriptures. I.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.32pt]
OH Book! infinite sweetnesse! let my heart
[.i65]##{nbsp}##Suck ev'ry letter, and a hony gain,
[.i270]##{nbsp}##Precious for any grief in any part;
To cleare the breast, to mollifie all pain.

[.v,margin-top=11.34pt]
Thou art all health, health thriving, till it make
[.i270]##{nbsp}##A full eternitie: thou art a masse
Of strange delights, where we may wish & take,
Ladies, look here; this is the thankfull glasse,

[.v,margin-top=11.63pt]
That mends the lookers eyes: this is the well
[.i270]##{nbsp}##That washes what it shows. Who can indeare
[.i270]##{nbsp}##Thy praise too much? thou art heav'ns Lieger [.turn-down]##here,##
Working against the states of death and hell.

[.v,margin-top=11.35pt]
Thou art joyes handsell: heav'n lies flat in thee
[.i270]##{nbsp}##Subject to ev'ry mounters bended knee.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=12.60pt]
'''

[margin-top=3.71pt]
=== II.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.27pt]
OH that I knew how all thy lights combine,
[.i85]##{nbsp}##And the configurations of their glorie!
[.i295]##{nbsp}##Seeing not onely how each verse doth shine,
But all the constellations of the storie.

[.v,margin-top=13.37pt]
This verse marks that, and both do make a motion
[.i295]##{nbsp}##Unto a third, that ten leaves off doth lie:
[.i295]##{nbsp}##Then as dispersed herbs do watch a potion,
These three make up some Christians destinie:

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=4.05pt]
Such[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 59 (leaf 58)
[.v,margin-top=2.80pt]
Such are thy secrets, which my life makes good,
[.i220]##{nbsp}##And comments on thee: for in ev'ry thing
[.i220]##{nbsp}##Thy words do finde me out, & parallels bring,
And in another make me understood.

[.v,margin-top=-0.15pt]
[.i220]##{nbsp}##Starres are poore books, & oftentimes do misse:
[.i220]##{nbsp}##This book of starres lights to eternall blisse.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=6.73pt]
'''

[margin-top=2.35pt]
=== ¶ Whitsunday.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.94pt]
LIsten sweet Dove unto my song,
And spread thy golden wings in me;
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Hatching my tender heart so long,
Till it get wing, and flie away with thee.

[.v,margin-top=11.08pt]
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Where is that fire which once descended
[.i95]##{nbsp}##On thy Apostles? thou didst then
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Keep open house, richly attended,
Fasting all comers by twelve chosen men.

[.v,margin-top=11.16pt]
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Such glorious gifts thou didst bestow,
[.i95]##{nbsp}##That th' earth did like a heav'n appeare;
[.i95]##{nbsp}##The starres were coming down to know
If they might mend their wages, and serve here.

[.v,margin-top=11.30pt]
[.i95]##{nbsp}##The sunne, which once did shine alone,
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Hung down his head, and wisht for night,
[.i95]##{nbsp}##When he beheld twelve sunnes for one
Going about the world, and giving light.

[.v,margin-top=11.57pt]
[.i95]##{nbsp}##But since those pipes of gold, which brought
[.i95]##{nbsp}##That cordiall water to our ground,
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Were cut and martyr'd by the fault
Of those, who did themselves through their side wound

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.14pt]
Thou[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1110]##{nbsp}##C 2

endif::[]

<<<

// page 60 (leaf 59)
[.v,margin-top=-1.10pt]
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Thou shutt'st the doore, and keep'st within;
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Scarce a good joy creeps through the chink:
[.i95]##{nbsp}##And if the braves of conqu'ring sinne
Did not excite thee, we should wholly sink.

[.v,margin-top=7.62pt]
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Lord, though we change, thou art the same;
[.i95]##{nbsp}##The same sweet God of love and light:
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Restore this day, for thy great name,
Unto his ancient and miraculous right.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=11.09pt]
'''

[margin-top=0.97pt]
=== ¶ Grace.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.90pt]
MY stock lies dead, and no increase
Doth my dull husbandrie improve:
O let thy graces without cease
[.i770]##{nbsp}##Drop from above!

[.v,margin-top=7.80pt]
If still the sunne should hide his face,
Thy house would but a dungeon prove,
Thy works nights captives: O let grace
[.i770]##{nbsp}##Drop from above!

[.v,margin-top=7.48pt]
The dew doth ev'ry morning fall;
And shall the dew out-strip thy dove?
The dew, for which grasse cannot call,
[.i770]##{nbsp}##Drop from above.

[.v,margin-top=7.62pt]
Death is still working like a mole,
And digs my grave at each remove:
Let grace work too, and on my soul
[.i770]##{nbsp}##Drop from above.

[.v,margin-top=7.62pt]
Sinne is still hammering my heart
Unto a hardnesse, void of love:
Let suppling grace, to crosse his art,
[.i770]##{nbsp}##Drop from above.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
O[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 61 (leaf 60)
[.v,margin-top=0.15pt]
O come! for thou dost know the way.
Or if to me thou wilt not move,
Remove me, where I need not say,
[.i755]##{nbsp}##Drop from above.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=4.04pt]
'''

[margin-top=-4.39pt]
=== ¶ Praise.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=-0.58pt]
TO write a verse or two, is all the praise,
[.i650]##{nbsp}##That I can raise:
[.i470]##{nbsp}##Mend my estate in any wayes,
[.i835]##{nbsp}##Thou shalt have more.

[.v,margin-top=3.50pt]
I go to Church; help me to wings, and I
[.i835]##{nbsp}##Will thither flie;
[.i470]##{nbsp}##Or, if I mount unto the skie,
[.i835]##{nbsp}##I will do more.

[.v,margin-top=3.38pt]
Man is all weaknesse; there is no such thing
[.i835]##{nbsp}##As Prince or King:
[.i470]##{nbsp}##His arm is short; yet with a sling
[.i835]##{nbsp}##He may do more.

[.v,margin-top=2.75pt]
An herb destill'd, and drunk, may dwell next doore,
[.i835]##{nbsp}##On the same floore,
[.i470]##{nbsp}##To a brave soul: Exalt the poore,
[.i835]##{nbsp}##They can do more.

[.v,margin-top=3.42pt]
O raise me then! poore bees, that work all day,
[.i835]##{nbsp}##Sting my delay,
[.i470]##{nbsp}##Who have a work, as well as they,
[.i835]##{nbsp}##And much, much more.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=-0.14pt]
'''

[margin-top=-8.10pt]
=== ¶ Affliction.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=5.87pt]
KIll me not ev'ry day,
Thou Lord of life; since thy one death for me
[.i440]##{nbsp}##Is more then all my deaths can be,
[.i800]##{nbsp}##Though I in broken pay
Die over each houre of Methusalems stay.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
If[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1110]##{nbsp}##C 3

endif::[]

<<<

// page 62 (leaf 61)
[.v,margin-top=-0.25pt]
[.i895]##{nbsp}##If all mens tears were let
Into one common sewer, sea, and brine;
[.i455]##{nbsp}##What were they all, compar'd to thine
[.i895]##{nbsp}##Wherein if they were set,
They would discolour thy most bloudie sweat.

[.v,margin-top=5.48pt]
[.i895]##{nbsp}##Thou art my grief alone,
Thou Lord conceal it not: and as thou art
[.i510]##{nbsp}##All my delight, so all my smart:
[.i895]##{nbsp}##Thy crosse took up in one;
By way of imprest, all my future mone.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=1.46pt]
'''

[margin-top=-5.58pt]
=== ¶ Mattens.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=4.69pt]
I[.i315]##{nbsp}##Cannot ope mine eyes,
[.i100]##{nbsp}##But thou art ready there to catch
[.i220]##{nbsp}##My morning-soul and sacrifice:
Then we must needs for that day make a match.

[.v,margin-top=5.22pt]
[.i435]##{nbsp}##My God, what is a heart?
[.i220]##{nbsp}##Silver, or gold, or precious stone,
[.i220]##{nbsp}##Or starre, or rainbow, or a part
Of all these things, or all of them in one?

[.v,margin-top=5.74pt]
[.i435]##{nbsp}##My God, what is a heart,
[.i220]##{nbsp}##That thou shouldst it so eye, and wooe,
[.i220]##{nbsp}##Powring upon it all thy art,
As if that thou hadst nothing els to do?

[.v,margin-top=5.30pt]
[.i435]##{nbsp}##Indeed mans whole estate
[.i220]##{nbsp}##Amounts (and richly) to serve thee:
[.i220]##{nbsp}##He did not heav'n and earth create,
Yet studies them, not him by whom they be.

[.v,margin-top=5.67pt]
[.i435]##{nbsp}##Teach me thy love to know;
[.i220]##{nbsp}##That this new light, which now I see,
[.i220]##{nbsp}##May both the work and workman show:
Then by a sunne-beam I will climbe to thee.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
¶ Sinne.[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 63 (leaf 62)
[margin-top=1.94pt]
=== ¶ Sinne.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.31pt]
O That I could a sinne once see!
We paint the devil foul, yet he
[.i305]##{nbsp}##Hath some good in him, all agree.
Sinne is flat opposite to th' Almighty, seeing
It wants the good of __vertue,__ and of __being.__

[.v,margin-top=7.10pt]
[.i175]##{nbsp}##But God more care of us hath had:
[.i175]##{nbsp}##If apparitions make us sad,
[.i175]##{nbsp}##By sight of sinne we should grow mad.
[.i70]##{nbsp}##Yet as in sleep we see foul death, and live:
So devils are our sinnes in perspective.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=4.59pt]
'''

[margin-top=1.35pt]
=== ¶ Even-song.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=4.40pt]
B[.i535]##{nbsp}##Lest be the God of love,
[.i135]##{nbsp}##Who gave me eyes, and light, and power this day,
[.i325]##{nbsp}##Both to be busie, and to play.
[.i325]##{nbsp}##But much more blest be God above,

[.v,margin-top=8.91pt]
[.i725]##{nbsp}##Who gave me sight alone,
[.i325]##{nbsp}##Which to himself he did denie:
[.i325]##{nbsp}##For when he sees my waies, I die:
But I have got his sonne, and he hath none.

[.v,margin-top=7.39pt]
[.i725]##{nbsp}##What have I brought thee home
For this thy love? have I discharg'd the debt,
[.i325]##{nbsp}##Which this dayes favour did beget?
[.i400]##{nbsp}##I ranne; but all I brought, was fome.

[.v,margin-top=7.29pt]
[.i725]##{nbsp}##Thy diet, care, and cost
[.i325]##{nbsp}##Do end in bubbles, balls of winde;
[.i325]##{nbsp}##Of winde to thee whom I have crost,
But balls of wilde-fire to my troubled minde.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
Yet[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1110]##{nbsp}##C 4

endif::[]

<<<

// page 64 (leaf 63)
[.v,margin-top=-0.25pt]
[.i785]##{nbsp}##Yet still thou goest on,
And now with darknesse closest wearie eyes,
[.i540]##{nbsp}##Saying to man, __It doth suffice:__
[.i540]##{nbsp}##Henceforth repose; your work is done.

[.v,margin-top=11.15pt]
[.i785]##{nbsp}##Thus in thy Ebony box
[.i540]##{nbsp}##Thou dost inclose us, till the day
[.i540]##{nbsp}##Put our amendment in our way,
And give new wheels to our disorder'd clocks.

[.v,margin-top=11.71pt]
[.i785]##{nbsp}##I muse, which shows more love;
The day or night: that is the gale, this th'harbour;
[.i540]##{nbsp}##That is the walk, and this the arbour;
[.i540]##{nbsp}##Or that the garden, this the grove.

[.v,margin-top=11.09pt]
[.i785]##{nbsp}##My God, thou art all love.
[.i540]##{nbsp}##Not one poore minute scapes thy breast,
[.i540]##{nbsp}##But brings a favour from above;
And in this love, more then in bed, I rest.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=3.91pt]
'''

[margin-top=-2.82pt]
=== ¶ Church-monuments.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=5.42pt]
WHile that my soul repairs to her devotion,
Here I intombe my flesh, that it betimes
May take acquaintance of this heap of dust;
To which the blast of deaths incessant motion,
Fed with the exhalation of our crimes,
Drives all at last. Therefore I gladly trust

[.v,margin-top=5.81pt]
My bodie to this school, that it may learn
To spell his elements, and finde his birth
Written in dustie heraldrie and lines;
Which dissolution sure doth best discern,
Comparing dust with dust, and earth with earth.
These laugh at Ieat, and Marble put for signes,

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
To[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 65 (leaf 64)
[.v,margin-top=0.52pt]
To sever the good fellowship of dust,
And spoil the meeting. What shall point out them,
When they shall bow, and kneel, and fall down flat
To kisse those heaps, which now they have in trust?
Deare flesh, while I do pray, learn here thy stemme
And true descent; that when thou shalt grow fat,

[.v,margin-top=5.81pt]
And wanton in thy cravings, thou mayst know,
That flesh is but the glasse, which holds the dust
That measures all our time; which also shall
Be crumbled into dust. Mark here below
How tame these ashes are, how free from lust,
That thou mayst fit thy self against thy fall.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=4.85pt]
'''

[margin-top=-1.31pt]
=== ¶ Church-musick.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=-1.09pt]
SWeetest of sweets, I thank you: when displeasure
[.i225]##{nbsp}##Did through my bodie wound my minde,
You took me thence, and in your house of pleasure
[.i410]##{nbsp}##A daintie lodging me assign'd.

[.v,margin-top=5.55pt]
Now I in you without a bodie move,
[.i410]##{nbsp}##Rising and falling with your wings:
We both together sweetly live and love,
[.i410]##{nbsp}##Yet say sometimes, __God help poore Kings.__

[.v,margin-top=5.97pt]
Comfort, 'Ile die; for if you poste from me,
[.i410]##{nbsp}##Sure I shall do so, and much more:
But if I travell in your companie,
[.i410]##{nbsp}##You know the way to heavens doore.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=4.32pt]
'''

[margin-top=-1.65pt]
=== ¶ Church-lock and key.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=-0.62pt]
I Know it is my sinne, which locks thine eares,
And bindes thy hands,
Out-crying my requests, drowning my tears;
Or else the chilnesse of my faint demands.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
But[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1110]##{nbsp}##C 5

endif::[]

<<<

// page 66 (leaf 65)
[.v,margin-top=-0.59pt]
But as cold hands are angrie with the fire,
[.i935]##{nbsp}##And mend it still;
So I do lay the want of my desire,
Not on my sinnes, or coldnesse, but thy will.

[.v,margin-top=7.14pt]
Yet heare, O God, onely for his blouds sake
[.i850]##{nbsp}##Which pleads for me:
For though sinnes plead too, yet like stones they make
His blouds sweet current much more loud to be.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=6.14pt]
'''

[margin-top=1.72pt]
=== ¶ The Church-floore.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=1.93pt]
MArk you the floore? that square & speckled stone
[.i430]##{nbsp}##Which looks so firm and strong,
[.i1265]##{nbsp}##Is __Patience:__

[.v,margin-top=7.59pt]
And th'other black and grave, wherewith each one
[.i685]##{nbsp}##Is checker'd all along,
[.i1265]##{nbsp}##Humilitie:

[.v,margin-top=7.07pt]
The gentle rising, which on either hand
[.i265]##{nbsp}##Leads to the Quire above,
[.i1265]##{nbsp}##Is __Confidence:__

[.v,margin-top=7.47pt]
But the sweet cement, which in one sure band
[.i685]##{nbsp}##Ties the whole frame, is __Love__
[.i1100]##{nbsp}##And __Charitie.__

[.v,margin-top=7.36pt]
[.i265]##{nbsp}##Hither sometimes Sinne steals, and stains
[.i265]##{nbsp}##The marbles neat and curious veins:
But all is cleansed when the marble weeps.
[.i265]##{nbsp}##Sometimes Death, puffing at the doore,
[.i265]##{nbsp}##Blows all the dust about the floore:
But while he thinks to spoil the room, he sweeps.
[.i265]##{nbsp}##Blest be the __Architect,__ whose art
Could build so strong in a weak heart.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=-0.38pt]
¶ The[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 67 (leaf 66)
[margin-top=2.75pt]
=== ¶ The Windows.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=4.76pt]
LOrd, how can man preach thy eternall word?
[.i240]##{nbsp}##He is a brittle crazie glasse:
Yet in thy temple thou dost him afford
[.i430]##{nbsp}##This glorious and transcendent place,
[.i430]##{nbsp}##To be a window, through thy grace.

[.v,margin-top=7.21pt]
But when thou dost anneal in glasse thy storie,
[.i430]##{nbsp}##Making thy life to shine within
The holy Preachers; then the light and glorie
[.i430]##{nbsp}##More rev'rend grows, & more doth wine
[.i430]##{nbsp}##Which else shows watrish, bleak, & thin.

[.v,margin-top=7.40pt]
Doctrine and life, colours and light, in one
[.i430]##{nbsp}##When they combine and mingle, bring
A strong regard and aw: but speech alone
[.i430]##{nbsp}##Doth vanish like a flaring thing,
[.i430]##{nbsp}##And in the eare, not conscience ring.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=13.92pt]
'''

[margin-top=3.27pt]
=== ¶ Trinitie Sunday.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.31pt]
LOrd, who hast form'd me out of mud,
And hast redeem'd me through thy bloud,
[.i190]##{nbsp}##And sanctifi'd me to do good;

[.v,margin-top=11.60pt]
Purge all my sinnes done heretofore:
[.i190]##{nbsp}##For I confesse my heavie score,
[.i190]##{nbsp}##And I will strive to sinne no more.

[.v,margin-top=11.49pt]
Enrich my heart, mouth, hands in me,
[.i95]##{nbsp}##With faith, with hope, with charitie;
[.i190]##{nbsp}##That I may runne, rise, rest with thee.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=1.62pt]
¶ Con-[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 68 (leaf 67)
[margin-top=5.36pt]
=== ¶ Content.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=9.08pt]
PEace mutt'ring thoughts, and do not grudge to keep
[.i305]##{nbsp}##Within the walls of your own breast:
Who cannot on his own bed sweetly sleep,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Can on anothers hardly rest.

[.v,margin-top=7.36pt]
Gad not abroad at ev'ry quest and call
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Of an untrained hope or passion.
To court each place or fortune that doth fall,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Is wantonnesse in contemplation.

[.v,margin-top=7.69pt]
Mark how the fire in flints doth quiet lie,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Content and warm t' it self alone:
But when it would appeare to others eye,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Without a knock it never shone.

[.v,margin-top=7.40pt]
Give me the pliant minde, whose gentle measure
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Complies and suits with all estates;
Which can let loose to a crown, and yet with pleasure
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Take up within a cloisters gates.

[.v,margin-top=7.88pt]
This soul doth span the world, and hang content
[.i490]##{nbsp}##From either pole unto the centre:
Where in each room of the well-furnisht tent
[.i490]##{nbsp}##He lies warm, and without adventure.

[.v,margin-top=7.58pt]
The brags of life are but a nine dayes wonder;
[.i490]##{nbsp}##And after death the fumes that spring
From private bodies, make as big a thunder,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##As those which rise from a huge King.

[.v,margin-top=7.95pt]
Onely thy Chronicle is lost; and yet
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Better by worms be all once spent,
Then to have hellish moths still gnaw and fret
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Thy name in books, which may not rent:

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
When[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 69 (leaf 68)
[.v,margin-top=-3.42pt]
When all thy deeds, whose brunt thou feel'st alone,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Are chaw'd by others pens and tongue;
And as their wit is, their digestion,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Thy nourisht fame is weak or strong.

[.v,margin-top=5.88pt]
Then cease discoursing soul, till thine own ground,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Do not thy self or friends importune.
He that by seeking hath himself once found,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Hath euer found a happie fortune.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=3.87pt]
'''

[margin-top=-1.24pt]
=== ¶ The Quidditie.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=1.01pt]
MY God, a verse is not a crown,
No point of honour, or gay suit,
No hawk, or banquet, or renown,
Nor a good sword, nor yet a lute:

[.v,margin-top=5.70pt]
It cannot vault, or dance, or play;
It never was in __France__ or __Spain__;
Nor can it entertain the day
With a great stable or demain:

[.v,margin-top=5.67pt]
It is no office, art, or news,
Nor the Exchange, or busie Hall;
But it is that which while I use
I am with thee, and __Most take all.__

[.poem-rule,margin-top=2.05pt]
'''

[margin-top=-2.55pt]
=== ¶ Humilitie.

[.v.dropcap.p976,margin-top=2.41pt]
I Saw the Vertues sitting hand in hand
In sev'rall ranks upon an azure throne,
Where all the beasts and fowls by their command
Presented tokens of submission.
Humilitie, who sat the lowest there
[.i970]##{nbsp}##To execute their call,
When by the beasts the presents tendred were,
[.i1205]##{nbsp}##Gave them about to all.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.81pt]
The[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 70 (leaf 69)
[.v.p952,margin-top=-3.19pt]
The angrie Lion did present his paw,
Which by consent was giv'n to Mansuetude.
The fearfull Hare her eares, which by their law
Humilitie did reach to Fortitude.
The jealous Turkie brought his corall-chain;
[.i890]##{nbsp}##That went to Temperance.
On Justice was bestow'd the Foxes brain,
[.i890]##{nbsp}##Kill'd in the way by chance.

[.v.p956,margin-top=5.92pt]
At length the Crow bringing the Peacocks plume,
(For he would not) as they beheld the grace
Of that brave gift, each one began to fume,
And challenge it, as proper to his place,
Till they fell out: which when the beasts espied,
[.i480]##{nbsp}##They leapt upon the throne;
And if the Fox had liv'd to rule their side,
[.i890]##{nbsp}##They had depos'd each one.

[.v.p955,margin-top=5.93pt]
Humilitie, who held the plume, at this
Did weep so fast, that the tears trickling down
Spoil'd all the train: then saying, __Here it is__
__For which ye wrangle,__ made them turn their frown
Against the beasts: so joyntly bandying,
[.i890]##{nbsp}##They drive them soon away;
And then amerc'd them, double gifts to bring
[.i890]##{nbsp}##At the next Session-day.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=3.57pt]
'''

[margin-top=-1.81pt]
=== ¶ Frailtie.

[.v.dropcap.p956,margin-top=2.47pt]
LOrd, in my silence how do I despise
What upon trust
Is styled __honour, riches,__ or __fair__ __eyes__;
[.i840]##{nbsp}##But is __fair__ __dust__!
[.i290]##{nbsp}##I surname them __guilded clay,__
[.i290]##{nbsp}##__Deare earth, fine grasse__ or __hay__;
In all, I think my foot doth ever tread
[.i125]##{nbsp}##Upon their head.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.91pt]
But[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 71 (leaf 70)
[.v.p952,margin-top=4.25pt]
But when I view abroad both Regiments;
[.i780]##{nbsp}##The worlds, and thine:
Thine clad with simplenesse, and sad events;
[.i780]##{nbsp}##The other fine,
[.i305]##{nbsp}##Full of glorie and gay weeds,
[.i385]##{nbsp}##Brave language, braver deeds:
That which was dust before, doth quickly rise,
[.i810]##{nbsp}##And prick mine eyes.

[.v.p944,margin-top=11.81pt]
O brook not this, lest if what even now
[.i780]##{nbsp}##My foot did tread,
Affront those joyes, wherewith thou didst endow,
[.i780]##{nbsp}##And long since wed
[.i305]##{nbsp}##My poore soul, ev'n sick of love:
[.i385]##{nbsp}##It may a Babel prove
Commodious to conquer heav'n and thee
[.i780]##{nbsp}##Planted in me.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=16.75pt]
'''

[margin-top=9.16pt]
=== ¶ Constancie.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=7.34pt]
W[.i210]##{nbsp}##Ho is the honest man?
He that doth still and strongly good pursue,
To God, his neighbour, and himself most true:
[.i315]##{nbsp}##Whom neither force nor fawning can
Unpinne, or wrench from giving all their due.

[.v,margin-top=10.82pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Whose honestie is not
So loose or easie, that a ruffling winde
Can blow away, or glittering look it blinde:
[.i315]##{nbsp}##Who rides his sure and even trot,
While the world now rides by, now lags behinde.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
Who,[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 72 (leaf 71)
[.v,margin-top=-3.24pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Who, when great trials come,
Nor seeks, nor shunnes them; but doth calmly stay,
Till he the thing and the example weigh:
[.i305]##{nbsp}##All being brought into a summe,
What place or person calls for, he doth pay.

[.v,margin-top=5.74pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Whom none can work or wooe
To use in any thing a trick or sleight,
For above all things he abhorres deceit:
[.i305]##{nbsp}##His words and works and fashion too
All of a piece, and all are cleare and straight.

[.v,margin-top=5.66pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Who never melts or thaws
At close tentations: when the day is done,
His goodnesse sets not, but in dark can runne:
[.i305]##{nbsp}##The sunne to others writeth laws,
And is their vertue; Vertue is his Sunne.

[.v,margin-top=5.71pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Who, when he is to treat
With sick folks, women, those whom passions sway,
Allows for that, and keeps his constant way:
[.i305]##{nbsp}##Whom others faults do not defeat;
But though men fail him, yet his part doth play.

[.v,margin-top=5.44pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Whom nothing can procure,
When the wide world runnes bias, from his will
To writhe his limbes, and share, not mend the ill.
[.i305]##{nbsp}##This is the Mark-man, safe and sure,
Who still is right, and prayes to be so still.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=2.85pt]
'''

[margin-top=-3.27pt]
=== ¶ Affliction.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=3.80pt]
MY heart did heave, and there came forth, __O God!__
By that I knew that thou wast in the grief,
To guide and govern it to my relief,
[.i345]##{nbsp}##Making a scepter of the rod:
[.i495]##{nbsp}##Hadst thou not had thy part,

[.v,margin-top=3.49pt]
Sure the unruly sigh had broke my heart.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
But[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 73 (leaf 72)
[.v,margin-top=1.81pt]
But since thy breath gave me both life and shape,
Thou knowst my tallies; and when there's assign'd
So much breath to a sigh, what's then behinde?
[.i300]##{nbsp}##Or if some yeares with it escape,
[.i470]##{nbsp}##The sigh then onely is
Gale to bring me sooner to my blisse.

[.v,margin-top=7.28pt]
Thy life on earth was grief, and thou art still
Constant unto it, making it to be
A point of honour, now to grieve in me,
[.i300]##{nbsp}##And in thy members suffer ill.
[.i470]##{nbsp}##They who lament one crosse,
Thou dying daily, praise thee to thy losse.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=6.27pt]
'''

[margin-top=3.10pt]
=== __¶__ The Starre.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=4.98pt]
BRight spark, shot from a brighter place,
Where beams surround my Saviours face,
[.i430]##{nbsp}##Canst thou be any where
[.i570]##{nbsp}##So well as there?

[.v,margin-top=7.07pt]
[.i115]##{nbsp}##Yet, if thou wilt from thence depart,
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Take a bad lodging in my heart;
[.i500]##{nbsp}##For thou canst make a debter,
[.i645]##{nbsp}##And make it better.

[.v,margin-top=7.36pt]
[.i190]##{nbsp}##First with thy fire-work burn to dust
Folly, and worse then folly, lust:
[.i605]##{nbsp}##Then with thy light refine,
[.i745]##{nbsp}##And make it shine.

[.v,margin-top=7.40pt]
[.i305]##{nbsp}##So disengag'd from sinne and sicknesse,
[.i430]##{nbsp}##Touch it with thy celestiall quicknesse,
[.i720]##{nbsp}##That it may hang and move
[.i870]##{nbsp}##After thy love.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=-0.93pt]
Then[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 74 (leaf 73)
[.v,margin-top=8.41pt]
Then with our trinitie of light,
[.i90]##{nbsp}##Motion, and heat, let's take our flight
[.i310]##{nbsp}##Unto the place where thou
[.i475]##{nbsp}##Before didst bow.

[.v,margin-top=12.81pt]
Get me a standing there, and place
[.i90]##{nbsp}##Among the beams, which crown the face
[.i310]##{nbsp}##Of him, who dy'd to part
[.i475]##{nbsp}##Sinne and my heart:

[.v,margin-top=12.71pt]
That so among the rest I may
[.i90]##{nbsp}##Glitter, and curle, and winde as they:
[.i310]##{nbsp}##That winding is their fashion
[.i475]##{nbsp}##Of adoration.

[.v,margin-top=12.63pt]
Sure thou wilt joy, by gaining me
[.i90]##{nbsp}##To flie home like a laden bee
[.i310]##{nbsp}##Unto that hive of beams
[.i475]##{nbsp}##And garland-streams.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=14.57pt]
'''

[margin-top=7.12pt]
=== ¶ Sunday.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=13.53pt]
O Day most calm, most bright,
The fruit of this, the next worlds bud,
Th' indorsement of supreme delight,
Writ by a friend, and with his bloud;
The couch of time; cares balm and bay:
The week were dark, but for thy light:
[.i385]##{nbsp}##Thy torch doth show the way.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
The[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 75 (leaf 74)
[.v,margin-top=9.62pt]
[.i395]##{nbsp}##The other dayes and thou
Make up one man; whose face thou art,
Knocking at heaven with thy brow:
The worky-daies are the back-part;
The burden of the week lies there,
Making the whole to stoup and bow,
[.i345]##{nbsp}##Till thy release appeare.

[.v,margin-top=16.31pt]
[.i345]##{nbsp}##Man had straight forward gone
To endlesse death: but thou dost pull
And turn us round to look on one,
Whom, if we were not very dull,
We could not choose but look on still;
Since there is no place so alone,
[.i395]##{nbsp}##The which he doth not fill.

[.v,margin-top=15.46pt]
[.i395]##{nbsp}##Sundaies the pillars are,
On which heav'ns palace arched lies:
The other dayes fill up the spare
And hollow room with vanities.
They are the fruitfull beds and borders
In Gods rich garden: that is bare,
[.i535]##{nbsp}##Which parts their ranks and orders.

[.v,margin-top=16.13pt]
[.i600]##{nbsp}##The Sundaies of mans life,
Thredded together on times string,
Make bracelets to adorn the wife
Of the eternall glorious King.
On Sunday heavens gate stands ope;
Blessings are plentifull and rife,
[.i795]##{nbsp}##More plentifull then hope.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
This[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 76 (leaf 75)
[.v,margin-top=10.54pt]
[.i310]##{nbsp}##This day my Saviour rose,
And did inclose this light for his:
That, as each beast his manger knows,
Man might not of his fodder misse.
Christ hath took in this piece of ground,
And made a garden there for those
[.i310]##{nbsp}##Who want herbs for their wound.

[.v,margin-top=15.94pt]
[.i310]##{nbsp}##The rest of our Creation
Our great Redeemer did remove
With the same shake, which at his passion
Did th' earth and all things with it move.
As Samson bore the doores away,
Christs hands, though nail'd, wrought our salvation,
[.i310]##{nbsp}##And did unhinge that day.

[.v,margin-top=14.62pt]
[.i310]##{nbsp}##The brightnesse of that day
We sullied by our foul offence:
Wherefore that robe we cast away,
Having a new at his expense,
Whose drops of bloud paid the full price,
That was requir'd to make us gay,
[.i310]##{nbsp}##And fit for Paradise.

[.v,margin-top=15.09pt]
[.i310]##{nbsp}##Thou art a day of mirth:
And where the week-dayes trail on ground,
Thy flight is higher, as thy birth.
O let me take thee at the bound,
Leaping with thee from sev'n to seven,
Till that we both, being toss'd from earth,
[.i310]##{nbsp}##Flie hand in hand to heaven!

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
¶ Avarice.[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 77 (leaf 76)
[margin-top=4.85pt]
=== ¶ Avarice.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=3.36pt]
MOney, thou bane of blisse, & sourse of wo,
Whence com'st thou, that thou art so fresh and [.turn-up]##fine?##
[.i285]##{nbsp}##I know thy parentage is base and low:
Man found thee poore and dirtie in a mine.

[.v,margin-top=7.58pt]
Surely thou didst so little contribute
[.i95]##{nbsp}##To this great kingdome, which thou now hast got,
[.i95]##{nbsp}##That he was fain, when thou wert destitute,
To digge thee out of thy dark cave and grot:

[.v,margin-top=7.62pt]
Then forcing thee, by fire he made thee bright:
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Nay, thou hast got the face of man; for we
Have with out stamp and seal transferr'd our right:
Thou art the man, and man but drosse to thee.

[.v,margin-top=7.29pt]
[.i95]##{nbsp}##Man calleth thee his wealth, who made thee rich;
[.i95]##{nbsp}##And while he digs out thee, falls in the ditch.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=10.07pt]
'''

[margin-top=-1.79pt]
=== Anagram. MARY ARMY

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=13.72pt]
HOw well her name an __Army__ doth present,
In whom the __Lord of hosts__ did pitch his tent

[.poem-rule,margin-top=4.20pt]
'''

[margin-top=2.07pt]
=== ¶ To all Angels and Saints.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.61pt]
OH glorious spirits, who after all your bands
See the smooth face of God, without a frown
[.i880]##{nbsp}##Or strict commands;
Where ev'ry one is king, and hath his crown,
If not upon his head, yet in his hands:

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=1.06pt]
Not[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 78 (leaf 77)
[.v,margin-top=-3.35pt]
Not out of envie or maliciousnesse
Do I forbear to crave your speciall aid:
[.i870]##{nbsp}##I would addresse
My vows to thee most gladly, blessed Maid,
And Mother of my God, in my distresse.

[.v,margin-top=5.66pt]
Thou art the holy mine, whence came the gold,
The great restorative for all decay
[.i870]##{nbsp}##In young and old;
Thou art the cabinet where the jewell lay:
Chiefly to thee would I my soul unfold:

[.v,margin-top=5.49pt]
But now (alas!) I dare not; for our King,
Whom we do all joyntly adore and praise,
[.i870]##{nbsp}##Bids no such thing:
And where his pleasure no injunction layes,
('Tis your own case) ye never move a wing.

[.v,margin-top=4.89pt]
All worship is prerogative, and a flower
Of his rich crown, from whom lyes no appeal
[.i870]##{nbsp}##At the last houre:
Therefore we dare not from his garland steal,
To make a posie for inferiour power.

[.v,margin-top=5.44pt]
Although then others court you, if ye know
What's done on earth, we shall not fare the worse,
[.i870]##{nbsp}##Who do not so;
Since we are ever ready to disburse,
If any one our Masters hand can show.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=4.56pt]
'''

[margin-top=1.87pt]
=== ¶ Employment.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=2.33pt]
HE that is weary, let him sit.
My soul would stirre
And trade in courtesies and wit,
[.i715]##{nbsp}##Quitting the furre
To cold complexions needing it.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
Man[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 79 (leaf 78)
[.v,margin-top=-3.24pt]
Man is no starre, but a quick coal
[.i715]##{nbsp}##Of mortall fire:
Who blows it not, nor doth controll
[.i715]##{nbsp}##A faint desire,
Lets his own ashes choke his soul.

[.v,margin-top=4.93pt]
When th' elements did for place contest
[.i715]##{nbsp}##With him, whose will
Ordain'd the highest to be best;
[.i715]##{nbsp}##The earth sat still,
And by the others is opprest.

[.v,margin-top=5.26pt]
Life is a businesse, not good cheer;
[.i715]##{nbsp}##Ever in warres.
The sunne still shineth there or here,
[.i715]##{nbsp}##Whereas the starres
Watch an advantage to appeare.

[.v,margin-top=5.04pt]
Oh that I were an Orenge-tree,
[.i715]##{nbsp}##That busie plant!
Then should I ever laden be,
[.i715]##{nbsp}##And never want
Some fruit for him that dressed me.

[.v,margin-top=4.85pt]
But we are still too young or old;
[.i715]##{nbsp}##The man is gone,
Before we do our wares unfold:
[.i715]##{nbsp}##So we freez on,
Untill the grave increase our cold.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=6.34pt]
'''

[margin-top=1.71pt]
=== ¶ Deniall.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=4.91pt]
WHen my devotions could not pierce
Thy silent eares;
Then was my heart broken, as was my verse:
[.i310]##{nbsp}##My breast was full of fears
[.i715]##{nbsp}##And disorder:

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
My[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 80 (leaf 79)
[.v,margin-top=-3.46pt]
[.i345]##{nbsp}##My bent thoughts, like a brittle bow,
[.i1105]##{nbsp}##Did flie asunder:
[.i165]##{nbsp}##Each took his way; some would to pleasures go,
[.i450]##{nbsp}##Some to the warres and thunder
[.i1045]##{nbsp}##Of alarms.

[.v,margin-top=7.17pt]
[.i285]##{nbsp}##As good go any where, they say,
[.i1045]##{nbsp}##As to benumme
[.i65]##{nbsp}##Both knees and heart, in crying night and day,
[.i390]##{nbsp}##Come, come, my God, O come,
[.i970]##{nbsp}##But no hearing.

[.v,margin-top=6.89pt]
[.i165]##{nbsp}##O that thou shouldst give dust a tongue
[.i970]##{nbsp}##To crie to thee,
And then not heare it crying! all day long
[.i345]##{nbsp}##My heart was in my knee,
[.i970]##{nbsp}##But no hearing.

[.v,margin-top=6.95pt]
[.i165]##{nbsp}##Therefore my soul lay out of sight,
[.i560]##{nbsp}##Untun'd, unstrung:
My feeble spirit, unable to look right,
[.i345]##{nbsp}##Like a nipt blossome, hung
[.i970]##{nbsp}##Discontented.

[.v,margin-top=6.81pt]
[.i165]##{nbsp}##O cheer and tune my heartlesse breast,
[.i970]##{nbsp}##Deferre no time;
[.i165]##{nbsp}##That so thy favours granting my request,
[.i345]##{nbsp}##They and my minde may chime,
[.i970]##{nbsp}##And mend my ryme.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=10.48pt]
'''

[margin-top=1.70pt]
=== ¶ Christmas.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=3.18pt]
ALl after pleasures as I rid one day,
My horse and I, both tir'd, bodie and minde,
[.i355]##{nbsp}##With full crie of affections, quite astray;
[.i95]##{nbsp}##I took up in the next inne I could finde.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
There[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 81 (leaf 80)
[.v,margin-top=-0.44pt]
[.i55]##{nbsp}##There when I came, whom found I but my deare,
[.i325]##{nbsp}##My dearest Lord, expecting till the grief
[.i325]##{nbsp}##Of pleasures brought me to him, readie there
[.i55]##{nbsp}##To be all passengers most sweet relief?

[.v,margin-top=13.85pt]
[.i55]##{nbsp}##O Thou, whose glorious, yet contracted light,
Wrapt in nights mantle, stole into a manger;
Since my dark soul and brutish is thy right,
[.i55]##{nbsp}##To Man of all beasts be not thou a stranger.

[.v,margin-top=13.92pt]
Furnish & deck my soul, that thou mayst have
[.i380]##{nbsp}##A better lodging, then a rack, or grave.

[.v.p968,margin-top=16.55pt]
[.i55]##{nbsp}##THe shepherds sing; and shall I silent be?
[.i595]##{nbsp}##My God, no hymne for thee?
[.i55]##{nbsp}##My soul's a shepherd too; a flock it feeds
[.i550]##{nbsp}##Of thoughts, and words, and deeds;
[.i55]##{nbsp}##The pasture is thy word: the streams, thy grace
[.i550]##{nbsp}##Enriching all the place.
[.i55]##{nbsp}##Shepherd and flock shall sing, and all my powers
[.i550]##{nbsp}##Out-sing the day-light houres.
[.i55]##{nbsp}##Then we will chide the sunne for letting night
[.i550]##{nbsp}##Take up his place and right:
[.i55]##{nbsp}##We sing one common Lord; wherefore he should
[.i550]##{nbsp}##Himself the candle hold.
[.i55]##{nbsp}##I will go searching, till I finde a sunne
[.i550]##{nbsp}##Shall stay, till we have done;
[.i55]##{nbsp}##A willing shiner, that shall shine as gladly,
[.i550]##{nbsp}##As frost-nipt sunnes look sadly.
[.i55]##{nbsp}##Then we will sing, and shine all our own day,
[.i490]##{nbsp}##And one another pay:
[.i55]##{nbsp}##His beams shall cheer my breast, and both so twine,
[.i55]##{nbsp}##Till ev'n his beams sing, and my musick shine.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.85pt]
¶ Un-[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1145]##{nbsp}##D

endif::[]

<<<

// page 82 (leaf 81)
[margin-top=1.24pt]
=== ¶ Ungratefulnesse.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=5.09pt]
LOrd, with what bountie and rare clemencie
[.i220]##{nbsp}##Hast thou redeem'd us from the grave!
[.i485]##{nbsp}##If thou hadst let us runne,
[.i405]##{nbsp}##Gladly had man ador'd the sunne,
[.i485]##{nbsp}##And thought his god most brave;
Where now we shall be better gods then he.

[.v,margin-top=7.21pt]
Thou hast but two rare cabinets full of treasure,
[.i405]##{nbsp}##The __Trinitie,__ and __Incarnation:__
[.i485]##{nbsp}##Thou hast unlockt them both,
[.i405]##{nbsp}##And made them jewels to betroth
[.i485]##{nbsp}##The work of thy creation
Unto thy self in everlasting pleasure.

[.v,margin-top=7.10pt]
The statelier cabinet is the __Trinitie,__
[.i405]##{nbsp}##Whose sparkling light accesse denies:
[.i485]##{nbsp}##Therefore thou dost not show
[.i405]##{nbsp}##This fully to us, till death blow
[.i485]##{nbsp}##The dust into our eyes:
For by that powder thou wilt make us see.

[.v,margin-top=7.58pt]
But all thy sweets are packt up in the other;
[.i405]##{nbsp}##Thy mercies thither flock and flow:
[.i485]##{nbsp}##That as the first affrights,
[.i405]##{nbsp}##This may allure us with delights;
[.i485]##{nbsp}##Because this box we know;
For we have all of us just such another.

[.v,margin-top=7.28pt]
But man is close, reserv'd, and dark to thee:
[.i405]##{nbsp}##When thou demandest but a heart,
[.i485]##{nbsp}##He cavils instantly.
[.i405]##{nbsp}##In his poore cabinet of bone
[.i485]##{nbsp}##Sinnes have their box apart,
Defrauding thee, who gavest two for one.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
¶ Sighs[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 83 (leaf 82)
[margin-top=-0.35pt]
=== § Sighs and grones.

[.v,margin-top=5.21pt]
[.i805]##{nbsp}##O Do not use me
After my sinnes! look not on my desert,
But on thy glorie! then thou wilt reform
And not refuse me: for thou onely art
The mightie God, but I a sillie worm;
[.i805]##{nbsp}##O do not bruise me!

[.v,margin-top=7.21pt]
[.i805]##{nbsp}##O do not urge me!
For what account can thy ill steward make?
I have abus'd thy stock, destroy'd thy woods,
Suckt all thy magazens: my head did ake,
Till it found out how to consume thy goods:
[.i805]##{nbsp}##O do not scourge me!

[.v,margin-top=7.35pt]
[.i805]##{nbsp}##O do not blinde me!
I have deserv'd that an Egyptian night
Should thicken all my powers; because my lust
Hath still sow'd fig-leaves to exclude thy light:
But I am frailtie, and alreadie dust;
[.i805]##{nbsp}##O do not grinde me!

[.v,margin-top=7.36pt]
[.i805]##{nbsp}##O do not fill me
With the turn'd viall of thy bitter wrath!
For thou hast other vessels full of bloud,
A part whereof my Saviour empti'd hath,
Ev'n unto death: since he di'd for my good,
[.i805]##{nbsp}##O do not kill me!

[.v,margin-top=7.66pt]
[.i805]##{nbsp}##But O reprieve me!
For thou hast __life__ and __death__ at thy command;
Thou art both __Iudge__ and __Saviour, feast__ and __rod,__
__Cordiall__ and __Corrosive:__ put not thy hand
Into the bitter box; but O my God,
[.i805]##{nbsp}##My God, relieve me!

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
¶ The[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1110]##{nbsp}##D 2

endif::[]

<<<

// page 84 (leaf 83)
[margin-top=9.67pt]
=== ¶ The World.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=12.51pt]
LOve built a stately house; where __Fortune__ came:
And spinning phansies, she was heard to say,
That her fine cobwebs did support the frame,
Whereas they were supported by the same:
But __Wisdome__ quickly swept them all away.

[.v,margin-top=17.27pt]
Then __Pleasure__ came, who liking not the fashion,
Began to make __Balcones, Terraces,__
Till she had weakned all by alteration:
But rev'rend __laws,__ and many a __proclamation__
Reformed all at length with menaces.

[.v,margin-top=16.76pt]
Then enter'd __Sinne,__ and with that Sycomore,
Whose leaves first sheltred man from drought & dew,
Working and winding slily evermore,
The inward walls and Sommers cleft and tore:
But __Grace__ shor'd these, and cut that as it grew.

[.v,margin-top=16.83pt]
Then __Sinne__ combin'd with __Death__ in a firm band
To rase the building to the very floore:
Which they effected, none could them withstand.
But __Love__ and __Grace__ took __Glorie__ by the hand,
And built a braver Palace then before.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
Coloss.[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 85 (leaf 84)
[margin-top=6.51pt]
=== Coloss. 3. 3.

[.subhead,margin-top=4.57pt]
Our life is hid with Christ in God.

[.v.dropcap.p966,margin-top=13.33pt]
MY words & thoughts do both expresse this notiō
That __Life__ hath with the sun a double motion.
The first __Is__ straight, and our diurnall friend,
The other __Hid,__ and doth obliquely bend.
One life is wrapt __In__ flesh, and tends to earth.
The other winds towards __Him,__ whose happie birth
Taught me to live here so, __That__ still one eye
Should aim and shoot at that which __Is__ on high:
Quitting with daily labour all __MY__ pleasure,
To gain at harvest an eternall __Treasure.__

[.poem-rule,margin-top=10.26pt]
'''

[margin-top=2.37pt]
=== ¶ Vanitie.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=8.12pt]
T[.i170]##{nbsp}##He fleet Astronomer can bore,
And thred the spheres with his quick-piercing minde:
He views their stations, walks from doore to doore,
[.i360]##{nbsp}##Surveys, as if he had design'd
To make a purchase there: he sees their dances,
[.i555]##{nbsp}##And knoweth long before,
Both their full-ey'd aspects, and secret glances.

[.v,margin-top=12.07pt]
[.i360]##{nbsp}##The nimble Diver with his side
Cuts through the working waves, that he may fetch
His dearely-earned pearl, which God did hide
[.i360]##{nbsp}##On purpose from the ventrous wretch;
That he might save his life, and also hers,
[.i610]##{nbsp}##Who with excessive pride
Her own destruction and his danger wears.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
The[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1110]##{nbsp}##D 3

endif::[]

<<<

// page 86 (leaf 85)
[.v,margin-top=2.47pt]
[.i395]##{nbsp}##The subtil Chymick can devest
And strip the creature naked, till he finde
The callow principles within their nest:
[.i395]##{nbsp}##There he imparts to them his minde,
Admitted to their bed-chamber, before
[.i495]##{nbsp}##They appeare trim and drest
To ordinarie suitours at the doore.

[.v,margin-top=13.00pt]
[.i395]##{nbsp}##What hath not man sought out and found,
But his deare God? who yet his glorious law
Embosomes in us, mellowing the ground
[.i395]##{nbsp}##With showres and frosts, with love & aw,
So that we need not say, Where's this command?
[.i540]##{nbsp}##Poore man, thou searchest round
To finde out __death,__ but missest __life__ at hand.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=16.54pt]
'''

[margin-top=4.25pt]
=== ¶ Lent.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=8.81pt]
WElcome deare feast of Lent: who loves not thee,
He loves not Temperance, or Authoritie,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##But is compos'd of passion.
The Scriptures bid us __fast__; the Church sayes, __now:__
Give to thy Mother, what thou wouldst allow
[.i725]##{nbsp}##To ev'ry Corporation.

[.v,margin-top=13.18pt]
The humble soul compos'd of love and fear
Begins at home, and layes the burden there,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##When doctrines disagree.
He sayes, in things which use hath justly got,
I am a scandall to the Church, and not
[.i725]##{nbsp}##The Church is so to me.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
True[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 87 (leaf 86)
[.v,margin-top=0.33pt]
True Christians should be glad of an occasion
To use their temperance, seeking no evasion,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##When good is seasonable;
Unlesse Authoritie, which should increase
The obligation in us, make it lesse,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##And Power it self disable.

[.v,margin-top=11.23pt]
Besides the cleannesse of sweet abstinence,
Quick thoughts and motions at a small expense,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##A face not fearing light:
Whereas in fulnesse there are sluttish fumes,
Sowre exhalations, and dishonest rheumes,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##Revenging the delight.

[.v,margin-top=11.34pt]
Then those same pendant profits, which the spring
And Easter intimate, enlarge the thing,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##And goodnesse of the deed.
Neither ought other mens abuse of Lent
Spoil the good use; lest by that argument
[.i475]##{nbsp}##We forfeit all our Creed.

[.v,margin-top=11.19pt]
It's true, we cannot reach Christs forti'th day;
Yet to go part of that religious way,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##Is better then to rest:
We cannot reach our Saviours puritie;
Yet are we bid, __Be holy ev'n as he.__
[.i475]##{nbsp}##In both let's do our best.

[.v,margin-top=11.12pt]
Who goeth in the way which Christ hath gone,
Is much more sure to meet with him, then one
[.i475]##{nbsp}##That travelleth by-wayes:
Perhaps my God, though he be farre before,
May turn, and take me by the hand, and more
May strengthen my decayes.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
Yet[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1110]##{nbsp}##D 4

endif::[]

<<<

// page 88 (leaf 87)
[.v,margin-top=2.51pt]
Yet Lord instruct us to improve our fast
By starving sinne and taking such repast,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##As may our faults controll:
That ev'ry man may revell at his doore,
Not in his parlour; banquetting the poore,
[.i410]##{nbsp}##And among those his soul.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=15.71pt]
'''

[margin-top=6.37pt]
=== ¶ Vertue.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.90pt]
SWeet day, so cool, so calm, so bright,
The bridall of the earth and skie:
The dew shall weep thy fall to night;
[.i705]##{nbsp}##For thou must die.

[.v,margin-top=16.39pt]
Sweet rose, whose hue angrie and brave
Bids the rash gazer wipe his eye:
Thy root is ever in its grave,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##And thou must die.

[.v,margin-top=15.99pt]
Sweet spring, full of sweet dayes and roses,
A box where sweets compacted lie;
My musick shows ye have your closes,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##And all must die.

[.v,margin-top=17.20pt]
Onely a sweet and vertuous soul,
Like season'd timber, never gives;
But though the whole world turn to coal,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##Then chiefly lives.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
¶ The[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 89 (leaf 88)
[margin-top=3.63pt]
=== ¶ The Pearl. __Matth.__ 13.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=6.57pt]
I[.i160]##{nbsp}##Know the wayes of learning; both the head
[.i160]##{nbsp}##And pipes that feed the presse, and make it runne;
What reason hath from nature borrowed,
Or of it self, like a good huswife, spunne
In laws and policie; what the starres conspire,
[.i70]##{nbsp}##What willing nature speaks, what forc'd by fire;
[.i100]##{nbsp}##Both th' old discoveries, and the new-found seas,
[.i125]##{nbsp}##The stock and surplus, cause and historie:
[.i150]##{nbsp}##All these stand open, or I have the keyes:
[.i885]##{nbsp}##Yet I love thee.

[.v.p956,margin-top=13.68pt]
[.i235]##{nbsp}##I know the wayes of Honour, what maintains
[.i235]##{nbsp}##The quick returns of courtesie and wit:
In vies of favours whether partie gains,
[.i320]##{nbsp}##When glorie swells the heart, and moldeth it
[.i320]##{nbsp}##To all expressions both of hand and eye,
[.i320]##{nbsp}##Which on the world a true-love-knot may tie,
[.i320]##{nbsp}##And bear the bundle, wheresoe're it goes:
[.i320]##{nbsp}##How many drammes of spirit there must be
[.i320]##{nbsp}##To sell my life unto my friends or foes:
[.i1040]##{nbsp}##Yet I love thee.

[.v.p964,margin-top=13.07pt]
I know the wayes of Pleasure, the sweet strains,
[.i320]##{nbsp}##The lullings and the relishes of it;
[.i320]##{nbsp}##The propositions of hot bloud and brains;
What mirth and musick mean; what love and wit
Have done these twentie hundred yeares, and more:
[.i320]##{nbsp}##I know the projects of unbridled store:
My stuffe is flesh, not brasse; my senses live,
[.i320]##{nbsp}##And grumble oft, that they have more in me
Then he that curbs them, being but one to five:
Yet I love thee.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.87pt]
I[.i15]##{nbsp}##

[.foot-sig,margin-top=-9.62pt]
[.i1110]##{nbsp}##D 5

endif::[]

<<<

// page 90 (leaf 89)
[.v,margin-top=3.58pt]
I know all these, and have them in my hand:
Therefore not sealed, but with open eyes
I flie to thee, and fully understand
Both the main sale, and the commodities;
And at what rate and price I have thy love;
With all the circumstances that may move:
Yet through the labyrinths, not my groveling wit,
But thy silk twist let down from heav'n to me,
Did both conduct and teach me, how by it
[.i765]##{nbsp}##To climbe to thee.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=10.42pt]
'''

[margin-top=2.92pt]
=== ¶ Affliction.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=7.08pt]
BRoken in pieces all asunder,
[.i290]##{nbsp}##Lord, hunt me not,
[.i495]##{nbsp}##A thing forgot,
Once a poore creature, now a wonder,
[.i280]##{nbsp}##A wonder tortur'd in the space
[.i280]##{nbsp}##Betwixt this world and that of grace.

[.v,margin-top=11.78pt]
My thoughts are all a case of knives,
[.i495]##{nbsp}##Wounding my heart
[.i495]##{nbsp}##With scatter'd smart,
As watring pots give flowers their lives.
[.i280]##{nbsp}##Nothing their furie can controll,
[.i280]##{nbsp}##While they do wound and prick my soul.

[.v,margin-top=11.74pt]
All my attendants are at strife,
[.i460]##{nbsp}##Quitting their place
[.i460]##{nbsp}##Unto my face:
Nothing performs the task of life:
[.i280]##{nbsp}##The elements are let loose to fight,
[.i280]##{nbsp}##And while I live, trie out their right.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
Oh[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 91 (leaf 90)
[.v,margin-top=-0.11pt]
Oh help, my God! let not their plot
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Kill them and me,
[.i480]##{nbsp}##And also thee,
Who art my life: dissolve the knot,
[.i300]##{nbsp}##As the sunne scatters by his light
[.i300]##{nbsp}##All the rebellions of the night.

[.v,margin-top=5.15pt]
Then shall those powers, which work for grief,
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Enter thy pay,
[.i480]##{nbsp}##And day by day
Labour thy praise, and my relief;
[.i300]##{nbsp}##With care and courage building me,
[.i300]##{nbsp}##Till I reach heav'n, and much more thee.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=8.00pt]
'''

[margin-top=1.44pt]
=== ¶ Man.

[.v,margin-top=4.25pt]
[.i505]##{nbsp}##MY God, I heard this day,
That none doth build a stately habitation,
[.i265]##{nbsp}##But he that means to dwell therein.
[.i265]##{nbsp}##What house more stately hath there been,
Or can be, then is Man? to whose creation
[.i505]##{nbsp}##All things are in decay.

[.v,margin-top=5.37pt]
[.i505]##{nbsp}##For Man is ev'ry thing,
And more: He is a tree, yet bears no fruit;
[.i265]##{nbsp}##A beast, yet is, or should be more:
[.i265]##{nbsp}##Reason and speech we onely bring.
Parrats may thank us, if they are not mute,
[.i505]##{nbsp}##They go upon the score.

[.v,margin-top=5.51pt]
[.i505]##{nbsp}##Man is all symmetrie,
Full of proportions, one limbe to another,
[.i265]##{nbsp}##And all to all the world besides:
[.i265]##{nbsp}##Each part may call the farthest, brother:
For head with foot hath private amitie,
[.i505]##{nbsp}##And both with moons and tides.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
Nothing[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 92 (leaf 91)
[.v,margin-top=2.36pt]
[.i525]##{nbsp}##Nothing hath got so farre,
[.i25]##{nbsp}##But Man hath caught and kept it, as his prey.
[.i315]##{nbsp}##His eyes dismount the highest starre:
[.i315]##{nbsp}##He is in little all the sphere.
[.i25]##{nbsp}##Herbs gladly cure our flesh; because that they
[.i525]##{nbsp}##Finde their acquaintance there.

[.v,margin-top=13.85pt]
[.i525]##{nbsp}##For us the windes do blow,
The earth doth rest, heav'n move, and fountains flow.
[.i315]##{nbsp}##Nothing we see, but means our good,
[.i315]##{nbsp}##As our __delight,__ or as our __treasure:__
[.i25]##{nbsp}##The whole is, either our cupboard of __food,__
[.i525]##{nbsp}##Or cabinet of __pleasure.__

[.v,margin-top=13.73pt]
[.i525]##{nbsp}##The starres have us to bed;
[.i25]##{nbsp}##Night draws the curtain, which the sunne withdraws;
[.i315]##{nbsp}##Musick and light attend our head.
[.i315]##{nbsp}##All things unto our __flesh__ are kinde
[.i25]##{nbsp}##In their __descent__ and __being__; to our __minde__
[.i525]##{nbsp}##In their __ascent__ and __cause.__

[.v,margin-top=13.25pt]
[.i525]##{nbsp}##Each thing is full of dutie:
[.i25]##{nbsp}##Waters united are our navigation;
[.i315]##{nbsp}##Distinguished, our habitation;
[.i315]##{nbsp}##Below, our drink; above, our meat;
[.i25]##{nbsp}##Both are our cleanlinesse. Hath one such beautie?
[.i525]##{nbsp}##Then how are all things neat?

[.v,margin-top=14.47pt]
[.i525]##{nbsp}##More servants wait on Man,
[.i25]##{nbsp}##Then he'l take notice of: in ev'ry path
[.i315]##{nbsp}##He treads down that which doth befriend him,
[.i255]##{nbsp}##When sicknesse makes him pale and wan.
[.i25]##{nbsp}##Oh mightie love! Man is one world, and hath
[.i525]##{nbsp}##Another to attend him.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=3.16pt]
Since[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 93 (leaf 92)
[.v,margin-top=2.69pt]
[.i525]##{nbsp}##Since then, my God, thou hast
So brave a Palace built; O dwell in it,
[.i370]##{nbsp}##That it may dwell with thee at last!
[.i370]##{nbsp}##Till then, afford us so much wit;
That, as the world serves us, we may serve thee,
[.i525]##{nbsp}##And both thy servants be.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=12.38pt]
'''

[margin-top=5.72pt]
=== ¶ Antiphon.

[.v,margin-top=5.36pt]
__Chor.__ PRaised be the God of love,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Men.__ Here below,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Angels.__ And here above:
__Cho.__ Who hath dealt his mercies so,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Ang.__ To his friend,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Men.__ And to his foe;

[.v,margin-top=5.25pt]
__Cho.__ That both grace and glorie tend
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Ang.__ Us of old,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Men.__ And us in th'end.
__Cho.__ The great shepherd of the fold
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Ang.__ Us did make,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Men.__ For us was sold.

[.v,margin-top=5.63pt]
__Cho.__ He our foes in pieces brake;
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Ang.__ Him we touch;
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Men.__ And him we take.
__Cho.__ Wherefore since that he is such,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Ang.__ We adore,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Men.__ And we do crouch.

[.v,margin-top=5.85pt]
__Cho.__ Lord, thy praises should be more.
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Men.__ We have none,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##__Ang.__ And we no store.
__Cho.__ Praised be the God alone,
[.i705]##{nbsp}##Who hath made of two folds one.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
¶ Un-[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 94 (leaf 93)
[margin-top=6.28pt]
=== ¶ Unkindnesse.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=10.52pt]
LOrd, make me coy and tender to offend:
In friendship, first I think, if that agree,
[.i890]##{nbsp}##Which I intend,
[.i365]##{nbsp}##Unto my friends intent and end.
I would not use a friend, as I use Thee.

[.v,margin-top=15.20pt]
If any touch my friend, or his good name;
It is my honour and my love to free
[.i890]##{nbsp}##His blasted fame
[.i405]##{nbsp}##From the least spot or thought of blame.
I could not use a friend, as I use Thee.

[.v,margin-top=16.17pt]
My friend may spit upon my curious floore:
Would he gave gold? I lend it instantly;
[.i890]##{nbsp}##But let the poore,
[.i405]##{nbsp}##And thou within them starve at doore.
I cannot use a friend, as I use Thee.

[.v,margin-top=14.88pt]
When that my friend pretendeth to a place,
I quit my interest, and leave it free:
[.i890]##{nbsp}##But when thy grace
[.i405]##{nbsp}##Sues for my heart, I thee displace,
Nor would I use a friend, as I use Thee.

[.v,margin-top=11.41pt]
Yet can a friend what thou hast done fulfill?
O write in brasse, __My God upon a tree__
[.i890]##{nbsp}##His bloud did spill
[.i405]##{nbsp}##Onely to purchase my good-will:
Yet use I not my foes, as I use thee.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
¶ Life.[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 95 (leaf 94)
[margin-top=2.78pt]
=== ¶ Life.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=7.68pt]
I Made a posie, while the day ran by:
Here will I smell my remnant out, and tie
[.i885]##{nbsp}##My life within this band.
But time did becken to the flow'rs, and they
By noon most cunningly did steal away,
[.i885]##{nbsp}##And wither'd in my hand.

[.v,margin-top=5.77pt]
My hand was next to them, and then my heart:
I took, without more thinking, in good part
[.i885]##{nbsp}##Times gentle admonition:
Who did so sweetly deaths sad taste convey,
Making my minde to smell my fatall day;
[.i885]##{nbsp}##Yet sugring the suspicion.

[.v,margin-top=5.04pt]
Farewell deare flow'rs; sweetly your time ye spent,
Fit, while ye liv'd, for smell or ornament,
[.i885]##{nbsp}##And after death for cures.
I follow straight without complaints or grief,
Since if my sent be good, I care not, if
[.i95]##{nbsp}##It be as short as yours.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=11.94pt]
'''

[margin-top=4.14pt]
=== ¶ Submission.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=8.38pt]
BUt that thou art my wisdome, Lord,
And both mine eyes are thine,
My minde would be extreamly stirr'd
[.i155]##{nbsp}##For missing my designe.

[.v,margin-top=5.59pt]
Were it not better to bestow
[.i155]##{nbsp}##Some place and power on me?
Then should thy praises with me grow,
[.i155]##{nbsp}##And share in my degree.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
But[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 96 (leaf 95)
[.v,margin-top=-3.61pt]
But when I thus dispute and grieve,
[.i165]##{nbsp}##I do resume my sight,
And pilfring what I once did give,
[.i165]##{nbsp}##Disseize thee of thy right.

[.v,margin-top=5.67pt]
How know I, if thou shouldst me raise,
[.i165]##{nbsp}##That I should then raise thee?
Perhaps great places and thy praise
[.i165]##{nbsp}##Do not so well agree.

[.v,margin-top=5.85pt]
Wherefore unto my gift I stand;
[.i165]##{nbsp}##I will no more advise:
Onely do thou lend me a hand,
[.i165]##{nbsp}##Since thou hast both mine eyes.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=6.64pt]
'''

[margin-top=0.93pt]
=== ¶ Justice.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=5.46pt]
I Cannot skill of these thy wayes.
__Lord, thou didst make me, yet thou woundest me;__
__Lord, thou dost wound me, yet thou dost relieve me:__
__Lord, thou relievest, yet I die by thee:__
__Lord, thou dost kill me, yet thou dost reprieve me.__

[.v,margin-top=5.52pt]
[.i195]##{nbsp}##But when I mark my life and praise,
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Thy justice me most fitly payes:
__For, I do praise thee, yet I praise thee not:__
__My prayers mean thee, yet my prayers stray:__
__I would do well, yet sinne the hand hath got:__
__My soul doth love thee, yet it loves delay.__
[.i255]##{nbsp}##I cannot skill of these my wayes.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=7.19pt]
'''

[margin-top=4.17pt]
=== ¶ Charms and Knots.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=3.25pt]
WHo reade a chapter when they rise,
Shall ne're be troubled with ill eyes.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=-4.33pt]
A[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 97 (leaf 96)
[.v,margin-top=-0.51pt]
A poore mans rod, when thou dost ride,
Is both a weapon and a guide.

[.v,margin-top=5.67pt]
Who shuts his hand, hath lost his gold:
Who opens it, hath it twice told.

[.v,margin-top=5.93pt]
Who goes to bed and doth not pray,
Maketh two nights to ev'ry day.

[.v,margin-top=5.78pt]
Who by aspersions throw a stone
At th' head of others, hit their own.

[.v,margin-top=5.67pt]
Who looks on ground with humble eyes,
Findes himself there, and seeks to rise.

[.v,margin-top=5.97pt]
When th' hair is sweet through pride or lust,
The powder doth forget the dust.

[.v,margin-top=5.67pt]
Take one from ten, and what remains?
Ten still, if sermons go for gains.

[.v,margin-top=5.67pt]
In shallow waters heav'n doth show;
But who drinks on, to hell may go.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=6.37pt]
'''

[margin-top=1.83pt]
=== ¶ Affliction.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=4.77pt]
M[.i160]##{nbsp}##Y God, I read this day,
That planted Paradise was not so firm,
As was and is thy floting Ark; whose stay
And anchor thou art onely, to confirm
[.i335]##{nbsp}##And strengthen it in ev'ry age,
[.i335]##{nbsp}##When waves do rise, and tempests rage.

[.v,margin-top=5.59pt]
[.i420]##{nbsp}##At first we liv'd in pleasure;
Thine own delights thou didst to us impart:
When we grew wanton, thou didst use displeasure
To make us thine: yet that we might not part,
[.i335]##{nbsp}##As we at first did board with thee,
[.i335]##{nbsp}##Now thou wouldst taste our miserie.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
There[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 98 (leaf 97)
[.v,margin-top=2.18pt]
[.i450]##{nbsp}##There is but joy and grief;
If either will convert us, we are thine:
Some Angels us'd the first; if our relief
Take up the second, then thy double line
[.i280]##{nbsp}##And sev'rall baits in either kinde
[.i280]##{nbsp}##Furnish thy table to thy minde.

[.v,margin-top=5.92pt]
[.i450]##{nbsp}##Affliction then is ours;
We are the trees, whom shaking fastens more,
While blustring windes destroy the wanton bowres,
And ruffle all their curious knots and store.
[.i280]##{nbsp}##My God, so temper joy and wo,
[.i280]##{nbsp}##That thy bright beams may tame thy bow.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=9.14pt]
'''

[margin-top=0.23pt]
=== ¶ Mortification.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=1.85pt]
H[.i195]##{nbsp}##Ow soon doth man decay!
When clothes are taken from a chest of sweets
[.i190]##{nbsp}##To swaddle infants, whose young breath
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Scarce knows the way;
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Those clouts are little winding sheets,
Which do consigne and send them unto death.

[.v,margin-top=7.32pt]
[.i385]##{nbsp}##When boyes go first to bed,
They step into their voluntarie graves,
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Sleep bindes them fast; onely their breath
[.i490]##{nbsp}##Makes them not dead:
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Successive nights, like rolling waves,
Convey them quickly, who are bound for death.

[.v,margin-top=5.55pt]
[.i385]##{nbsp}##When youth is frank and free,
And calls for musick, while his veins do swell,
[.i190]##{nbsp}##All day exchanging mirth and breath
[.i490]##{nbsp}##In companie;
[.i190]##{nbsp}##That musick summons to the knell,
Which shall befriend him at the house of death.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=-0.27pt]
When[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 99 (leaf 98)
[.v,margin-top=3.98pt]
[.i350]##{nbsp}##When man grows staid and wise,
Getting a house and home, where he may move
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Within the circle of his breath,
[.i475]##{nbsp}##Schooling his eyes;
[.i190]##{nbsp}##That dumbe inclosure maketh love
Into the coffin, that attends his death.

[.v,margin-top=7.40pt]
[.i350]##{nbsp}##When age grows low and weak,
Marking his grave, and thawing ev'ry yeare,
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Till all do melt, and drown his breath
[.i475]##{nbsp}##When he would speak;
[.i190]##{nbsp}##A chair or litter shows the biere,
Which shall convey him to the house of death.

[.v,margin-top=7.17pt]
[.i350]##{nbsp}##Man, ere he is aware,
Hath put together a solemnitie,
[.i190]##{nbsp}##And drest his herse, while he has breath
[.i475]##{nbsp}##As yet to spare:
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Yet Lord, instruct us so to die,
That all these dyings may be life in death.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=11.80pt]
'''

[margin-top=3.18pt]
=== ¶ Decay.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=5.02pt]
SWeet were the dayes, when thou didst lodge with [.turn-down]##Lot,##
Struggle with Jacob, fit with Gideon,
Advise with Abraham, when thy power could not
Encounter Moses strong complaints and mone:
[.i300]##{nbsp}##Thy words were then, __Let me alone.__

[.v,margin-top=7.29pt]
One might have sought and found thee presently
At some fair oak, or bush, or cave, or well:
Is my God this way? No, they would reply:
He is to Sinai gone, as we heard tell:
[.i300]##{nbsp}##List, ye may heare great Aarons bell.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
But[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 100 (leaf 99)
[.v,margin-top=4.20pt]
But now thou dost thy self immure and close
In some one corner of a feeble heart:
Where yet both Sinne and Satan, thy old foes,
Do pinch and straiten thee, and use much art
[.i325]##{nbsp}##To gain thy thirds and little part.

[.v,margin-top=7.51pt]
I see the world grows old, when as the heat
Of thy great love once spread, as in an urn
Doth closet up it self, and still retreat,
Cold sinne still forcing it, till it return,
[.i325]##{nbsp}##And calling Justice, all things burn.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=12.38pt]
'''

[margin-top=3.66pt]
=== ¶ Miserie.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=5.50pt]
LOrd, let the Angels praise thy name.
Man is a foolish thing, a foolish thing,
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Folly and Sinne play all his game.
His house still burns; and ye he still doth sing,
[.i625]##{nbsp}##__Man is but grasse,__
[.i440]##{nbsp}##__He knows it, fill the glasse.__

[.v,margin-top=7.14pt]
[.i165]##{nbsp}##How canst thou brook his foolishnesse?
Why he'l not lose a cup of drink for thee:
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Bid him but temper his excesse;
Not he: he knows, where he can better be,
[.i660]##{nbsp}##As he will swear,
[.i440]##{nbsp}##Then to serve thee in fear.

[.v,margin-top=7.50pt]
[.i195]##{nbsp}##What strange pollutions doth he wed,
And make his own? as if none knew, but he.
[.i195]##{nbsp}##No man shall beat into his head,
That thou within his curtains drawn canst see:
[.i625]##{nbsp}##They are of cloth,
[.i440]##{nbsp}##Where never yet came moth.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
The[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 101 (leaf 100)
[.v,margin-top=2.21pt]
[.i230]##{nbsp}##The best of men, turn but thy hand
[.i35]##{nbsp}##For one poore minute, stumble at a pinne:
[.i230]##{nbsp}##They would not have their actions scann'd,
[.i35]##{nbsp}##Nor any sorrow tell them that they sinne,
[.i670]##{nbsp}##Though it be small,
[.i515]##{nbsp}##And measure not their fall.

[.v,margin-top=13.55pt]
[.i230]##{nbsp}##They quarrell thee, and would give over
[.i35]##{nbsp}##The bargain made to serve thee: but thy love
[.i230]##{nbsp}##Holds them unto it, and doth cover
[.i35]##{nbsp}##Their follies with the wing of thy milde Dove,
[.i670]##{nbsp}##Not suff'ring those
[.i515]##{nbsp}##Who would, to be thy foes.

[.v,margin-top=13.33pt]
[.i230]##{nbsp}##My God, Man cannot praise thy name:
[.i35]##{nbsp}##Thou art all brightnesse, perfect puritie;
[.i230]##{nbsp}##The sunne holds down his head for shame,
[.i35]##{nbsp}##Dead with eclipses, when we speak of thee:
[.i670]##{nbsp}##How shall infection
[.i515]##{nbsp}##Presume on thy perfection?

[.v,margin-top=14.80pt]
[.i230]##{nbsp}##As dirtie hands foul all they touch,
And those things most, which are most pure and fine:
[.i230]##{nbsp}##So our clay hearts, ev'n when we crouch
[.i35]##{nbsp}##To sing thy praises, make them lesse divine.
[.i670]##{nbsp}##Yet either this,
[.i515]##{nbsp}##Or none thy portion is.

[.v,margin-top=13.77pt]
[.i230]##{nbsp}##Man cannot serve thee; let him go,
[.i35]##{nbsp}##And serve the swine: there, there is his delight:
[.i230]##{nbsp}##He doth not like this vertue, no;
[.i35]##{nbsp}##Give him his dirt to wallow in all night:
[.i670]##{nbsp}##These Preachers make
[.i515]##{nbsp}##His head to shoot and ake.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=1.61pt]
Oh[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 102 (leaf 101)
[.v,margin-top=4.02pt]
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Oh foolish man! where are thine eyes?
How hast thou lost them in a croud of eares?
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Thou pull'st the rug, and wilt not rise,
No not to purchase the whole pack of starres:
[.i665]##{nbsp}##There let them shine,
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Thou must go sleep, or dine.

[.v,margin-top=11.30pt]
[.i195]##{nbsp}##The bird that sees a daintie bowre
Made in the tree, where she was wont to sit,
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Wonders and sings, but not his power
Who made the arbour: this exceeds her wit.
[.i665]##{nbsp}##But Man doth know
[.i480]##{nbsp}##The spring, whence all things flow:

[.v,margin-top=11.52pt]
[.i195]##{nbsp}##And yet as though he knew it not,
His knowledge winks, and lets his humours reigne;
[.i195]##{nbsp}##They make his life a constant blot,
And all the bloud of God to run in vain.
[.i665]##{nbsp}##Ah wretch! what verse
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Can thy strange wayes rehearse?

[.v,margin-top=11.60pt]
[.i195]##{nbsp}##Indeed at first Man was a treasure,
A box of jewels, shop of rarities,
[.i195]##{nbsp}##A ring, whose posie was, __My pleasure:__
He was a garden in a Paradise:
[.i620]##{nbsp}##Glorie and grace
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Did crown his heart and face.

[.v,margin-top=11.59pt]
[.i195]##{nbsp}##But sinne hath fool'd him. Now he is
A lump of flesh, without a foot or wing
[.i195]##{nbsp}##To raise him to the glimpse of blisse:
A sick toss'd vessel, dashing on each thing;
[.i665]##{nbsp}##Nay, his own shelf:
[.i480]##{nbsp}##My God, I mean my self.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
¶ Jordan.[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 103 (leaf 102)
[margin-top=7.54pt]
=== ¶ Jordan.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=10.14pt]
WHen first my lines of heav'nly joyes made men[.turn-down]##tion,##
Such was their lustre, they did so excell,
That I sought out quaint words, and trim invention;
My thoughts began to burnish, sprout, and swell,
Purling with metaphors a plain intention,
Decking the sense, as if it were to sell.

[.v,margin-top=17.86pt]
Thousands of notions in my brain did runne,
Off'ring their service, if I were not sped:
I often blotted what I had begunne;
This was not quick enough, and that was dead.
Nothing could seem too rich to clothe the sunne,
Much lesse those joyes which trample on his head.

[.v,margin-top=12.85pt]
As flames do work and winde, when they ascend,
So did I weave my self into the sense.
But while I bustled, I might heare a friend
Whisper, __How wide is all this long pretence!__
__There is in love a sweetnesse ready penn'd:__
__Copie out onely that, and save expense.__

[.poem-rule,margin-top=10.17pt]
'''

[margin-top=6.87pt]
=== ¶ Prayer.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=8.15pt]
OF what an easie quick accesse,
My blessed Lord, art thou! how suddenly
[.i195]##{nbsp}##May our requests thine eare invade!
To shew that state dislikes not easinesse.
If I but lift mine eyes, my suit is made:
Thou canst no more not heare, then thou canst die.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.21pt]
Of[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 104 (leaf 103)
[.v,margin-top=4.06pt]
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Of what supreme almightie power
Is thy great arm which spans the east and west,
[.i305]##{nbsp}##And tacks the centre to the sphere!
By it do all things live their measur'd houre:
We cannot ask the thing, which is not there,
Blaming the shallownesse of our request.

[.v,margin-top=7.87pt]
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Of what unmeasurable love
Art thou possest, who, when thou couldst not die,
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Wert fain to take our flesh and curse,
And for our sakes in person sinne reprove,
That by destroying that which ty'd thy purse,
Thou mightst make way for liberalitie!

[.v,margin-top=7.36pt]
[.i190]##{nbsp}##Since then these three wait on thy throne,
__Ease, Power,__ and __Love__; I value prayer so,
[.i190]##{nbsp}##That were I to leave all but one,
Wealth, fame, endowments, vertues, all should go;
I and deare prayer would together dwell,
And quickly gain, for each inch lost, an ell.

[.poem-rule,margin-top=10.31pt]
'''

[margin-top=3.64pt]
=== ¶ Obedience.

[.v.dropcap,margin-top=9.55pt]
M[.i220]##{nbsp}##Y God, if writings may
Convey a Lordship any way
Whither the buyer and the seller please;
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Let it not thee displease,
If this poore paper do as much as they.

[.v,margin-top=8.10pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##On it my heart doth bleed
[.i195]##{nbsp}##As many lines, as there doth need
To passe it self and all it hath to thee.
[.i480]##{nbsp}##To which I do agree,
And here present it as my speciall deed.

ifdef::backend-pdf[]
[.foot-cw,margin-top=0.88pt]
If[.i15]##{nbsp}##

endif::[]

<<<

// page 105 (leaf 104)
[.v,margin-top=4.20pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##If that hereafter Pleasure
[.i360]##{nbsp}##Cavill, and claim her part and measure,
As if this passed with a reservation,
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Or some such words in fashion;
I here exclude the wrangler from thy treasure.

[.v,margin-top=10.20pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##O let thy sacred will
[.i310]##{nbsp}##All thy delight in me fulfill!
Let me not think an action mine own way,
[.i480]##{nbsp}##But as thy love shall sway,
Resigning up the rudder to thy skill.

[.v,margin-top=8.43pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Lord, what is man to thee,
That thou shouldst minde a rotten tree?
Yet since thou canst not choose but see my actions;
[.i480]##{nbsp}##So great are thy perfections,
Thou mayst as well my actions guide, as see.

[.v,margin-top=9.39pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Besides, thy death and bloud
[.i310]##{nbsp}##Show'd a strange love to all our good:
Thy sorrows were in earnest; no faint proffer,
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Or superficiall offer
Of what we might not take, or be withstood.

[.v,margin-top=10.05pt]
[.i480]##{nbsp}##Wherefore I all forgo:
[.i310]##{nbsp}##To one word onely I say, No:
Where in the deed there was an intimation
